No manual base64 or\n38\tcopied client digest is needed. `--include-document` adds one read of the exact\n39\tcreated revision, returning text or a decoded file path. Inspect it before editing.\n40\t\n41\t`--record` retains the command. Its new `.evidence` directory retains exact\n42\trequests/responses and decoded documents. The displayed `client_view` is the\n43\tclient's own view. Inspect outcomes and findings; read required omitted content.\n44\tDo not reread a whole receipt just to extract a small field already displayed.\n45\tA capture error after send can leave a committed effect. Reconcile its original\n46\tkey with `remote operation --key KEY --record ABSOLUTE_NEW_RECORD`.\n47\t\n48\t## Read one revision\n49\t\n50\t```text\n51\tremote read --typed --compact --record ABSOLUTE_NEW_RECORD --artifact ID --expected-revision REVISION --context UUID --context-version N\n52\t```\n53\t\n54\tFor an imported baseline, replace the context fields with `--baseline ID`.\n55\tReads take no `--operation-key`, `--expected-project-version` or `--evaluator-file`.\n56\tChoose the exact revision and context from the selected inputs or actual results.\n57\t\n58\t## Reads and discovery\n59\t\n60\t| Need | Operation |\n61\t| --- | --- |\n62\t| Service readiness and selected components | `remote status --record ABSOLUTE_NEW_RECORD` |\n63\t| Local wheel metadata | `identity` |\n64\t| Captured calls, reads and failures so far | `observations --record ABSOLUTE_NEW_RECORD`; retains the full snapshot and returns its field pointers, no content verdict |\n65\t| Complete released instruction sections | `instructions --section \"RESOURCE_ID#HEADING\"`; repeat for up to 12 sections |\n66\t| One unknown input location | `find-file BASENAME --under RELATIVE_DIRECTORY/` |\n67\t| One exact inventory entry | `lookup-file RELATIVE_NAME` |\n68\t| One saved JSON field | `read-json ABSOLUTE_FILE --pointer '\"/field\"'`; omit pointer for root; optional `--keys` |\n69\t| A raw base64 field | Add `--decode-base64` to that field read |\n70\t| One text file or independent reads together | `read-text ABSOLUTE_FILE [ABSOLUTE_FILE ...]`; up to eight selected inventoried inputs, the launcher-pinned task.md, or work files, at most 64 KiB output |\n71\t| Recover a truncated text read | `read-text ABSOLUTE_FILE --offset 0 --limit 4096`; one file, character offsets; continue from next_offset until complete is true |\n72\t\n73\tFor `instructions`, use canonical IDs such as `docs/engineering/harness/DRAFT_DEFINITIONS.md#read-this-when`. The earlier `released-resources/` inventory prefix also works. Request known sections together.\n74\tFor authoring checklists and the repeated released 0.22.1 continuation heading,\n75\tuse the exact selectors in the selected plugin's `change/references/hosted-drafts.md`.\n76\t\n77\tFor known paths, use them directly. Inventory names are relative to the staged\n78\tinputs directory: `released-resources/docs/engineering/ARTIFACT_AUTHORING.md`,\n79\t`source/src/greeting.py`, `client-help/create-artifact.txt`. **Do not add `inputs/`\n80\tto these names.** Native Read takes the absolute path, not an inventory name.\n81\tA digest or lookup is not a content read. Batch independent reads when their exact\n82\tpaths are known. Unknown headings must not be guessed;\n83\tread the named file once to locate its actual headings. Request known current\n84\tsections together and reuse unchanged content. A full inventory is not required.\n85\t\n86\tSource inspection and test execution are different actions. Drafting a verification\n87\tcontract describes future checks; it does not require running them. If the task\n88\tauthorizes execution, use [Execution tools](native-execution-tools.md).\n89\t\n90\t## Other operations\n91\t\n92\tRaw mode remains `remote OPERATION --request ABSOLUTE_FILE --record ABSOLUTE_NEW_RECORD`.\n93\tUse it for operations without typed input, or when explicitly checking a raw\n94\tcontract. Original schemas are under `reference/server/contracts/`. Selected\n95\tschema views use `schemas/remote-v1/OPERATION.json` or `schemas/read-v1/OPERATION.json`.\n96\tEach view retains its original pointer/digest. Lifecycle test-copy schemas are\n97\tseparate; do not load them for drafting alone. `--key` reads a receipt, not a document.\n98\t\n99\tUse `observations --record ABSOLUTE_NEW_RECORD` before the final report. Read its\n100\tselected fields as needed. `observed_content_reads` contains successful visible\n101\tread metadata; `file_reads` retains Claude Read attempts, including failures.\n102\tUse the observed trace instead of rebuilding it with inventory lookups. Missing\n103\treads remain unknown; rereading now does not prove an earlier read. The capture\n104\tmay be incomplete, and subsequent report writes are not yet counted. Keep the\n105\trecord path as evidence instead of copying every command. This supplies no draft\n106\ttext, content judgement or lifecycle decision.\n107\t\n108\tRetain failed calls and recoveries. Template admission is not a content review.\n109\tReport only inspected facts and actual outcomes; distinguish unresolved content\n110\tfrom a completed definition. Do not claim an exhaustive failure count from memory.\n111\t"
}

Create a new intent when the desired outcome changes.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"291e8274f0c400a3d2d1ecdd56f2c10d68ab6cb40f667076a034e44d87c05e42\"}, {\"heading\": \"design-simplicity\", \"title\": \"Design simplicity\", \"anchor\": \"design-simplicity\", \"start_line\": 21, \"end_line\": 58, \"content\": \"## Design simplicity\\n\\nFor unfinished work, `harnessctl validate-draft REPO --artifact ID --json`\\nreports whether one supported draft has an admissible shape. Only exact values\\nat the same type and field in this evaluator's canonical template, and missing\\nor empty required authoring links, can be reported as incomplete. Invalid\\nidentity, dates, metadata shapes, actual endpoints, lifecycle history and\\ndisposition are errors. A missing non-template ID is an error. Background\\nfindings remain visible; this result makes no whole-repository validity claim.\\nComplete the authoring checklist and pass ordinary approval gates before\\nrequesting approval. Draft admission grants no authority to write or approve.\\n\\nChoose the least complex design that satisfies the agreed requirements and\\nconstraints. Justify material added complexity by the need it serves and why\\na simpler approach is insufficient. Consider the effort to understand, build,\\ntest, operate and change the whole solution. Defer speculative needs. Review\\nboth the necessity of the specified behavior and the simplicity of its\\nimplementation. Preserve correctness and the project's required quality and\\nsafety properties.\\n\\nStart from the smallest complete solution. Each project supplies its agreed\\nusers, scale, operating conditions and quality constraints. Agreed future needs\\ncan matter; imagined flexibility alone is insufficient. Credible risk analysis\\ncan justify prevention before an incident. Fewer lines, shorter specifications\\nor fewer tests do not establish simplicity; moving work to users, operations\\nor dependencies also has a cost.\\n\\nApply the relevant questions below before definition approval and during design\\nand implementation review. Return unjustified obligations or design complexity\\nfor revision through the existing decision process. Explain material choices\\nin the existing specification, design or review: the need, why a simpler option\\nis insufficient and the added cost. Scale the explanation to the decision; a\\nsmall choice may need one sentence, a significant design may use its existing\\narchitecture decision. Trivial changes need no separate comparison document.\\n\\nThese are human review standards. They introduce no separate KISS artifact,\\nmandatory score, receipt, automated simplicity gate or additional approval.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"29e9b6296f39215c4b2ad36659c0674f0b20650682db9aa1187fc97871cfdb06\"}, {\"heading\": \"verification\", \"title\": \"verification\", \"anchor\": \"verification\", \"start_line\": 126, \"end_line\": 135, \"content\": \"## verification\\n\\n### Checklist\\n\\n- Independence: expected values derive from the requirements and specifications, never from candidate output.\\n- A requirement-to-evidence matrix with a pass condition per requirement.\\n- Cover normal behavior and meaningful failure outcomes or risks; do not invent failure scenarios to fill a template.\\n- What accepted behavior or quality does each test or CI check establish? Cover distinct relevant outcomes, reuse applicable evidence, and avoid mirroring every implementation branch or combination.\\n- Pass criteria name the platforms and the evaluator.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"e6d36e5fe7a23845861e67079317f856b4f892d7f17c4f3d4dbdb4476edb7720\"}]}]}",

A decision right describes which\n22\taction that person or agent is authorized to perform. Being a Human does not\n23\tgrant every right in this table. The graph lists the derived requirements.\\n- Put implementation details where they help the implementer; no required `can ... under` wording.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"ee6452eeeb618cbe10c941638e7f6be38d2709f53e169920c2d3d012c94ce978\"}, {\"heading\": \"requirement\", \"title\": \"requirement\", \"anchor\": \"requirement\", \"start_line\": 59, \"end_line\": 75, \"content\": \"## requirement\\n\\n### Checklist\\n\\n- State the observable behavior and why it is needed. Ordinary language is accepted; `SHALL` is optional.\\n- What agreed outcome does this obligation serve, for whom and under which conditions? Separate requirements from assumptions and speculative needs.\\n- Include a concrete acceptance condition in `Examples`, `Acceptance`, `measure`, `verification_notes`, or a linked verification contract.\\n- Name the source capability and a verification method. Supported method arrays contain `test`, `analysis`, `inspection`, or `demonstration`; retained text methods remain readable.\\n- Replace unfinished placeholders and resolve blocking decisions before approval.\\n\\n### Guidance\\n\\nWrite enough for someone to implement and check the behavior.     "content": "+++\nid = \"INT-P3-001\"\ntype = \"intent\"\ntitle = \"Verify the rehearsal greeting output\"\nstatus = \"draft\"\nowners = [\"test-owner\"]\ncreated = \"2026-10-10\"\nupdated = \"2026-10-10\"\noutcome = \"The test operator can verify that greeting() returns exactly Hello rehearsal.\"\n\n[relations]\n+++\n\n# Verify the rehearsal greeting output\n\n## In plain words\n\nThe test operator needs to confirm that the fixture greeting function produces\nthe expected output and to retain a complete engineering record of that check.\n\n## Problem\n\nThe disposable lifecycle rehearsal requires an independently verified greeting.\nWithout a test record, the operator cannot confirm the exact output or export\nevidence of the check.\n\n## Success measures\n\n| Measure | Today | When reached | Observed |\n| --- | --- | --- | --- |\n| Greeting assertion passes with exact match | not measured | Equals Hello rehearsal | Local test execution output |\n\n## Not this\n\n- Real product decisions or releases.\n- Changes to the greeting source code.\n- External deployment or publication.\n"
  }

    "content": "+++\nid = \"SPEC-P3-001\"\ntype = \"specification\"\ntitle = \"Rehearsal greeting fixture contract\"\nstatus = \"draft\"\nowners = [\"test-owner\"]\ncreated = \"2026-10-10\"\nupdated = \"2026-10-10\"\ncontract = \"The fixture function greeting() returns the string Hello rehearsal.\"\n\n[relations]\nspecifies = [\"REQ-P3-001\"]\n+++\n\n# Rehearsal greeting fixture contract\n\n## In plain words\n\nThe greeting function is a fixed fixture that always returns one exact string.\n\n## Scope\n\nThis specification governs the return value of `greeting()` in `src/greeting.py`.\nIt does not govern the function's interface, performance, or deployment.\n\n## Rules\n\n**P3-GREET-001.** The function `greeting()` MUST return the string `Hello rehearsal`.\n\n## Examples\n\n**Given** the fixture source is unchanged, **when** `greeting()` is called,\n**then** it returns `Hello rehearsal` (P3-GREET-001).\n\n## Coverage\n\n| Requirement | Rules |\n| --- | --- |\n| REQ-P3-001 | P3-GREET-001 |\n"
  }
}

Do not use absolute paths,\\nbackslashes, wildcards, dot components, drive prefixes, URIs, or duplicate\\ncase variants.\\n\\nApproval authorizes routine execution through the single procedure in\\n`docs/engineering/harness/AUTHORITY.md#authority-from-work-approval`. The selected person or\\nagent can start, implement, check, record completion and prepare required\\nverification within this scope without separate permission for those steps.\\nRecord completion only after the work and evidence are complete. Owner\\nacceptance and delivery remain distinct. Specify any authorized push/PR action\\nand destination in the decision envelope; do not imply merge or publication.\\n\\nAdd `architecture = [\\\"ARCH-xxx\\\", \\\"ADR-xxx\\\"]` under `[relations]` when architecture applies. The relation selects every applicable architecture plus every required deciding ADR. An ADR may be omitted only for a selected architecture whose accepted `decision_assessment` is `no_significant_decision`; every `adr_required` architecture needs at least one selected active ADR that decides it.\\n\\nAn architecture is applicable when it addresses an architecturally significant requirement implemented by this work order. Every selected architecture must conform to at least one of the selected specifications. Omit the `architecture` relation only when no active architecture addresses any implemented requirement. Routine requirements without an active `addresses` edge do not require fabricated architecture coverage. A present `architecture` relation must not be empty.\\n\\n## Objective\\n\\n## In scope\\n\\n## Out of scope\\n\\n## Authorized decision envelope\\n\\nWhat may the implementation agent decide locally?\\n\\n## Constraints\\n\\n## Expected change surface\\n\\nInspect the affected implementation and supporting paths before approval,\\nusing the selected released DRAFT_WORK_ORDERS.md procedure. Name each planned\\npath and its reason here. operating contract\\n\\n**Inputs:** A proposed release or ongoing operating obligation, its scope,\\nexisting work-order or requirement IDs, and any accepted contract that may\\nalready cover it.\\n\\n**Output:**\\n\\n- **Formal artifacts:** A completed release-contract (REL) or operating-contract\\n  (OPS) draft when needed. An accepted contract is reused only if its meaning\\n  and scope already cover the proposed obligation.\\n- **Transient working material:** Selection notes, new IDs and paths, and\\n  unresolved questions. No release or operating authorization is inferred.\\n\\n**Actions:**\\n\\n1. Decide which obligation applies: release scope uses `release_contract`;\\n   continuing assurance uses `operating_contract`. Do not create both by default.\\n2. Read existing contracts selected for reuse. If an accepted contract needs\\n   changed meaning, use the accepted-definition amendment boundary; do not edit\\n   it through this draft-creation procedure.\\n3. For a missing contract, use the supported domain and artifact creation\\n   sequence from \\u201cDefine the change.\\u201d Inspect the dry-run result and use its\\n   returned `allocated_id` in the creation command.\\n4. Complete the generated body and accountable metadata. For a REL, record\\n   release scope, version conditions, rollback, and evidence expectations.\\n   For an OPS, record measurable observability, support, and operating obligations.\\n5. Set `REL.gates` to every existing work-order ID covered by the release\\n   contract, or `OPS.assures` to every existing requirement ID for which it\\n   claims continuing assurance. Do not leave invented or unresolved IDs.\\n6. Apply `## release_contract` or `## operating_contract` and its `### Checklist`\\n   in `docs/engineering/ARTIFACT_AUTHORING.md`.\\n7. Validate the completed draft and resolve findings within the proposed scope.\\n8. Include the contract in [Authorize the work](AUTHORIZE_WORK.md#procedure) for the exact human decision.\\n\\n**Harness commands:**\\n\\nHere `TYPE` is `release_contract` or `operating_contract`, selected in action 1:\\n\\n```text\\nharnessctl create-artifact REPO --domain DOMAIN --type TYPE --dry-run --json\\nharnessctl create-artifact REPO --domain DOMAIN --type TYPE --id CONTRACT-ID --json\\nharnessctl validate REPO --json\\n```\\n\\n**Completion:** The applicable contract is covered by an unchanged accepted\\nrecord or a completed draft ready for its decision. Unresolved amendments\\nremain visible and block dependent work.\\n\\n**Later use:** An accepted REL governs release preparation. An accepted OPS\\ngoverns its stated continuing obligations after delivery. Perform the checks\\nand follow-up actions named in the OPS at its specified triggers; one successful\\ndelivery does not establish continuing conformance. External operating actions\\nstill require their exact authority.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"723a469b890666ae4b1e7b95a6231200a6794e6b792fef8e043ed82bc4f96ce5\"}]}]}",

Content\n48\tdigests bind the snapshot to the resulting baseline. Context heads, artifact\n49\trevisions, snapshot and receipt share one database transaction. Temporary Git\n50\trepositories are discarded after the result is accepted or refused.\n51\t\n52\tCapture first commits the exact input projection as test candidate P. P excludes\n53\tthe new VREC. The output retains the input hosted baseline B, source fixture S,\n54\tP and evaluator identity. Actual implementation candidate C is recorded separately\n55\tby qualification. Subsequent commits never alter the candidate stored in a VREC.\n56\t\n57\tExport selects an immutable baseline, checks its complete snapshot and Git bundle,\n58\tand returns exact bounded bytes. The installed client writes only to a new explicit\n59\tdestination and leaves an incomplete marker until every file hash is checked.\n60\tNo export writes to or synchronizes an authoritative repository.\n61\t\n62\t## Bounds and failure categories\n63\t\n64\tKeep 4 MiB requests, 2 MiB responses, 1 MiB individual file inputs, two concurrent\n65\tprojections, 120-second evaluator calls and 10,000 retained artifact revisions.\n66\tA pilot snapshot/export that exceeds the response bound is refused atomically.\n67\tRetained complete snapshots have a separate explicit project count bound of 128.\n68\tThis is a small private pilot, not an unbounded document store.\n69\t\n70\tBoundary/malformed requests, stale inputs, evaluator refusals, invalid output and\n71\tunknown transport outcomes remain distinct. Read the operation receipt after a\n72\tlost reply. A changed request under the same accepted key is a conflict. The\n73\tservice does not automatically rerun a mutation at a newer version.\n74\t\n75\t## Bounded Cypher reads\n76\t\n77\tThe existing `cypher` MCP/HTTP read selects one exact view. Its request uses the\n78\tread schema, not the lifecycle command schema. Read the public labels and\n79\tproperties in [the physical representation](../../docs/notes/hosted-artifact-graph.md#physical-representation)\n80\tbefore selecting a query. `Artifact` and `Revision` are distinct public labels.\n81\t\n82\tThe admitted form has one `MATCH` or `OPTIONAL MATCH` path, labelled nodes,\n83\toptional parameterized property comparisons, `RETURN`, and a numeric `LIMIT`.\n84\tFor example, with `parameters` containing a selected artifact ID under `artifact`:\n85\t\n86\t```cypher\n87\tMATCH (r:Revision) WHERE r.artifact_id = $artifact RETURN r.artifact_id AS id, r.status AS status LIMIT 10\n88\t```\n89\t\n90\tUse the response's completeness fields with the requested row/byte/depth budget.\n91\tThis example supplies no project filter: the service binds the selected project\n92\tand view. Unlabelled nodes, `WITH`, aggregation and arbitrary database syntax are\n93\tnot part of this small public read form. A rejected query has not completed a read.\n94\tKeep the exact refusal; consult the documented form before constructing another\n95\trequest. Do not weaken query admission or bypass it through a direct database call.\n96\t"
}

[Obtain the human's decision](AUTHORIZE_WORK.md#obtain-the-humans-decision).\n27\t4. [Apply the selected transitions](AUTHORIZE_WORK.md#apply-the-selected-transitions).\n28\t5. [Confirm the authorization result](AUTHORIZE_WORK.md#confirm-the-authorization-result).\n29\t\n30\t## Procedure\n31\t\n32\t### Assess the proposed artifact package\n33\t\n34\t**Inputs:** The selected work order (WO) IDs, proposed definitions, validation\n35\tresults, and existing decision records (DEC).\n36\t\n37\t**Output:**\n38\t\n39\t- **Formal artifacts:** None created or modified.\n40\t- **Transient working material:** A review list identifying the selected\n41\t  artifacts, their states, applicable checks, and reported blockers.\n42\t\n43\t**Actions:**\n44\t\n45\t1. Run validation for the repository.\n46\t2. Inspect each selected work order (WO) with the command below.\n47\t3. Read the work order (WO) and its returned reading manifest.\n48\t4. Review the governing definitions against the relevant checklists in\n49\t   `docs/engineering/ARTIFACT_AUTHORING.md`.\n50\t5. Check that the proposed work has defined scope, verification coverage,\n51\t   assurance classification, and accountable humans for the required decisions.\n52\t6. List the blockers affecting the selected package. Keep unrelated findings\n53\t   separate.\n54\t\n55\t**Harness commands:**\n56\t\n57\t```text\n58\tharnessctl validate REPO --json\n59\tharnessctl check REPO --artifact WO-ID --json\n60\tharnessctl check REPO --artifact WO-ID --checkpoint transition --target approved --json\n61\t```\n62\t\n63\tRead the result of each required check. A `pass` satisfies that check only;\n64\tit does not approve the artifacts or authorize implementation. If a required\n65\tcheck returns `fail` or `not_assessable`, report its identifier and stated\n66\treason, then follow “If a procedure is blocked.” Do not proceed with the\n67\taffected approval.\n68\t\n69\tThe last command evaluates work-order approval gates. A checkpoint-free\n70\t`check` only reports lifecycle context. Definition artifacts cannot be selected\n71\twith `check`; their transition gates are evaluated by the previews in [Apply the selected transitions](AUTHORIZE_WORK.md#apply-the-selected-transitions).\n72\t\n73\tBefore proposing a definition for approval, resolve its template placeholders.\n74\tIf it contains an `Open decisions` section, confirm that no unresolved decision\n75\tremains before setting that section to `None`. Do not remove an unresolved\n76\tquestion merely to make validation pass.\n77\t\n78\t**Completion:** The selected package has an explicit list of review findings\n79\tand approval blockers. No approval is inferred from these checks.\n80\t\n81\t**Later use:** [Resolve approval blockers](AUTHORIZE_WORK.md#resolve-approval-blockers) resolves the blockers. [Obtain the human's decision](AUTHORIZE_WORK.md#obtain-the-humans-decision) presents the package for a\n82\thuman decision.\n83\t\n84\t### Resolve approval blockers\n85\t\n86\t**Inputs:** The findings from [Assess the proposed artifact package](AUTHORIZE_WORK.md#assess-the-proposed-artifact-package) and the affected draft artifacts or\n87\tpending decisions (DEC).\n88\t\n89\t**Output:**\n90\t\n91\t- **Formal artifacts:** Corrected drafts within the proposed scope. Where an\n92\t  actual human decision is supplied, the selected decision (DEC) receives its\n93\t  disposition. A paired risk (RISK) may change through that same operation.\n94\t- **Transient working material:** The resolution of each finding and any\n95\t  remaining dependency requiring an amendment or another human decision.\n96\t\n97\t**Actions:**\n98\t\n99\t1. Correct incomplete or inconsistent draft content within the requested scope."

  "content": "20\tand work orders (WO) with completion recorded when the required checks pass.\n21\t\n22\t**Steps:**\n23\t\n24\t1. [Establish the execution context](EXECUTE_WORK.md#establish-the-execution-context).\n25\t2. [Start the selected work](EXECUTE_WORK.md#start-the-selected-work).\n26\t3. [Implement the approved scope](EXECUTE_WORK.md#implement-the-approved-scope).\n27\t4. [Retain the implementation evidence](EXECUTE_WORK.md#retain-the-implementation-evidence).\n28\t5. [Record implementation completion](EXECUTE_WORK.md#record-implementation-completion).\n29\t\n30\t## Procedure\n31\t\n32\t### Establish the execution context\n33\t\n34\t**Inputs:** The selected work order (WO) ID and its recorded approval.\n35\t\n36\t**Output:**\n37\t\n38\t- **Formal artifacts:** None changed.\n39\t- **Transient working material:** The current execution scope, reading list,\n40\t  applicable procedure, and trusted Git base for the complete change set.\n41\t\n42\t**Actions:**\n43\t\n44\t1. Inspect the selected work order (WO).\n45\t2. Read the formal artifacts in `context.reading_manifest`. For instructions,\n46\t   read the selected procedure's entry conditions, current step, and prerequisites\n47\t   whose conditions apply. Reuse current required material still in context;\n48\t   reread it when changed or lost. After compaction, obtain a fresh `check`\n49\t   result before continuing. This does not remove any required formal input.\n50\t3. Read the approved behavior, permitted paths, decision limits, stop\n51\t   conditions, verification contracts (VER), and required repository checks.\n52\t4. Identify the complete change baseline required by the selected procedure.\n53\t   Retain its full commit ID as `BASE`. Do not choose a newer base merely to\n54\t   exclude earlier changes from the checks.\n55\t5. Compare the planned work with the recorded approval. Return changed scope\n56\t   to definition and authorization before executing it.\n57\t\n58\t**Harness command:**\n59\t\n60\t```text\n61\tharnessctl check REPO --artifact WO-ID --json\n62\t```\n63\t\n64\t**Completion:** The executor has an explicit approved scope and the inputs\n65\trequired by the selected procedure. Missing baseline or scope information\n66\tis reported before dependent work.\n67\t\n68\t**Later use:** [Start the selected work](EXECUTE_WORK.md#start-the-selected-work), [Implement the approved scope](EXECUTE_WORK.md#implement-the-approved-scope), [Retain the implementation evidence](EXECUTE_WORK.md#retain-the-implementation-evidence), [Record implementation completion](EXECUTE_WORK.md#record-implementation-completion) use this scope, baseline, and verification plan.\n69\t\n70\t### Start the selected work\n71\t\n72\t**Inputs:** The approved work order (WO), actual executor identity, and\n73\tcurrent execution context.\n74\t\n75\t**Output:**\n76\t\n77\t- **Formal artifacts:** The selected work order (WO) becomes `in_progress`\n78\t  with a lifecycle-history entry when start succeeds.\n79\t- **Transient working material:** Start-check and transition results.\n80\t\n81\t**Actions:**\n82\t\n83\t1. If the work order (WO) is already `in_progress`, inspect the recorded start\n84\t   and resume unapplied work. Do not replay the start transition.\n85\t2. Otherwise, run start preflight under the existing approval.\n86\t3. Preview the transition to `in_progress` using the actual executor identity.\n87\t4. Apply the same transition only when preflight and the preview pass.\n88\t5. Inspect the resulting state.\n89\t\n90\t**Harness commands:**\n91\t\n92\t```text\n93\tharnessctl preflight REPO --work-order WO-ID --phase start --json\n94\tharnessctl transition REPO --set WO-ID=in_progress --decision WO-ID=ACTOR --json\n95\tharnessctl transition REPO --set WO-ID=in_progress --decision WO-ID=ACTOR --apply --json\n96\tharnessctl check REPO --artifact WO-ID --json\n97\t```\n98\t\n99\t**Completion:** The selected work order (WO) is `in_progress`, or its start\n100\tis blocked with an explicit reason. Existing approval covers a permitted\n101\tstart; no duplicate human start decision is required.\n102\t\n103\t**Later use:** [Implement the approved scope](EXECUTE_WORK.md#implement-the-approved-scope) performs the authorized implementation.\n104\t\n105\t### Implement the approved scope\n106\t\n107\t**Inputs:** The work order (WO) in `in_progress`, approved definitions,\n108\tpermitted paths, and baseline from [Establish the execution context](EXECUTE_WORK.md#establish-the-execution-context).\n109\t\n110\t**Output:**\n111\t\n112\t- **Repository files:** The code, tests, documentation, or configuration\n113\t  changes authorized by the work order (WO).\n114\t- **Formal artifacts:** No lifecycle transition in this step. Artifact content\n115\t  changes require the authority and procedure applicable to those records.\n116\t- **Transient working material:** Implementation notes and unresolved findings.\n117\t\n118\t**Actions:**\n119\t\n120\t1. Inspect the files and behavior to be changed.\n121\t2. Compare the complete actual and planned path set with the approved scope.\n122\t3. Run the scope check below with every actual and planned path.\n123\t4. Make the changes allowed by the work order (WO).\n124\t5. Run local checks as needed to guide the implementation. Keep failures\n125\t   available for the evidence record.\n126\t6. Review the diff against the accepted definitions. Apply\n127\t   [Review of implemented changes](../ARTIFACT_AUTHORING.md#review-of-implemented-changes).\n128\t7. Resolve in-scope defects. Stop affected work when a finding requires changed\n129\t   requirements, paths, or authority.\n130\t\n131\t**Harness commands:**\n132\t\n133\tRepeat `--changed-path PATH` for the complete set, including planned new files:\n134\t\n135\t```text\n136\tharnessctl check REPO --artifact WO-ID --checkpoint scope --changed-path PATH --changes-complete --json\n137\t```\n138\t\n139\tThis declaration is checked evidence, not independent proof of completeness."

Confirm that the reviewed artifact contents still match the human decision.\\n2. Select only the named transitions. Approve governing definitions before\\n   dependent work, or use one explicitly selected packet for mutually\\n   dependent definitions when the evaluator permits it.\\n3. Preview the transition command below.\\n4. Compare every selected ID, target state, actor, and reported effect with\\n   the human decision. Resolve failed gates before proceeding.\\n5. Recheck reviewed definition digests immediately before applying.\\n6. Repeat the passing command with `--apply`. Do not add related artifacts\\n   that the human did not select.\\n\\n**Harness commands:**\\n\\n```text\\nharnessctl transition REPO --set ARTIFACT-ID=TARGET --decision ARTIFACT-ID=ACTOR --json\\nharnessctl transition REPO --set ARTIFACT-ID=TARGET --decision ARTIFACT-ID=ACTOR --apply --json\\n```\\n\\nFor each clarification below the formal-decision threshold, add\\n`--reason \\\"ARTIFACT-ID=Question and confirmed answer\\\"` to both the preview\\nand apply command. Use the actual question and answer. When one artifact\\nhas several such answers, combine them into one reason value for that ID.\\nKeep each complete `ID=TEXT` value as one argument. Confirm the saved reason\\nin the resulting lifecycle entry before discarding the working notes.\\n\\nFor approval, `TARGET` is `approved`. For rejection, use `rejected` and add\\n`--reason \\\"ARTIFACT-ID=Rejection reason\\\"` to both commands. Rejection is\\nterminal. For a packet, repeat `--set` and `--decision` for every explicitly\\nselected artifact. Preview and apply must contain the same assignments.\\n\\n**Completion:** The evaluator reports the actual applied transitions, or the\\nspecific refusal. A successful preview alone changes nothing.\\n\\n**Later use:** [Confirm the authorization result](AUTHORIZE_WORK.md#confirm-the-authorization-result) confirms the resulting authority and readiness.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"c5215dec9f35d1a99bf6e4d658a594a31dc544131e70db49b1b8f79514e124b8\"}]}]}",
